## 33. Servidor Linux, parte 2: bwrap, «pásame la pantalla» y guía
- [ ] Sandbox de Linux con bwrap en sandbox/linux/: la llamada va detrás de cfg(target_os = "linux") y la función que arma el argv no lleva cfg, para probarla en cualquier plataforma. wrap envuelve con bwrap --unshare-net --die-with-parent, monta dentro <home>/net/proxy.sock y <home>/daemon.sock y arranca primero tirith net bridge, así que el agente sigue viendo el mismo HTTPS_PROXY. Los archivos, como el perfil de macOS del paso 27 (lo negado, tapado con tmpfs). Es el diseño del sandbox de Anthropic para Linux (bubblewrap y un puente a sockets Unix) con el binario de Tirith en lugar de socat. Comprobar si Chromium usa su propio sandbox dentro de bwrap y, si no, --no-sandbox como en macOS. Ubuntu 24.04 limita los user namespaces sin privilegios con AppArmor: documentar el ajuste si el runner lo necesita. Test bwrap_argv_has_no_network y, en CI Linux, linux_sandbox_blocks_direct_network: un curl dentro no sale sin el proxy y un dominio bloqueado se rechaza (check: `grep -rq 'fn bwrap_argv_has_no_network' rust/tirith && cargo test -q -p tirith bwrap_argv_has_no_network`)

Para un login, un código 2FA, un CAPTCHA o un pago, el bot abre una tarjeta account con un enlace a esa vista, que enseña su navegador en vivo (CDP Page.startScreencast por el puerto del paso 32) y le manda lo que el dueño teclea y pulsa (Input.dispatchKeyEvent, Input.dispatchMouseEvent); al cerrarla, el bot sigue en su siguiente turno. Solo con el token del dueño (paso 26). Test screencast_relay contra un CDP falso (check: `grep -rq 'fn screencast_relay' rust/tirith && cargo test -q -p tirith screencast_relay`)
- [ ] docs/SERVER.md y la sección «Always on» de la landing: Tirith en un VPS de la UE con un usuario tirith, scripts/install.sh, bwrap, Node y los CLIs de los agentes con su login por SSH, tirith service install (systemd de usuario y loginctl enable-linger), [server] mode = "server", la página por Tailscale o ssh -L 7717:127.0.0.1:7717 sin abrirla a internet y el móvil con tirith room qr. Si Muse no corre en Linux, la cadena de fallback de cada rol lo cubre (check: `test -f docs/SERVER.md && grep -q 'enable-linger' docs/SERVER.md && grep -q 'Always on' site/index.html`)

## 36. Connectors through an MCP gateway 1/7: identity and per-bot secrets

Only this connector line moves now. The old roadmap is stopped, and these are the two prerequisites the gateway cannot safely work without.
- [x] Caller identity comes from a token, never from self-declared headers. Today the CLI and hooks send X-Tirith-Lane and X-Tirith-Role, which a sandboxed caller can forge. Make wrap put its existing per-who proxy token in TIRITH_TOKEN; the CLI, tirith hook and installed HTTP hooks send it as X-Tirith-Token (including allowedEnvVars), and the daemon derives lane, project role and bot from that token alone. The owner gets 0600 <home>/owner.token, unreadable inside the sandbox; the page reaches the daemon through the same owner authentication rather than by having no headers. Tokenless hook calls from the owner's own agent sessions keep only today's block-list behavior; every other daemon route without a valid caller token is 403, except routes that already carry their own unforgeable token. Tests api_rejects_a_spoofed_role and api_rejects_calls_without_token cover a sandboxed caller claiming manager/owner and the owner path still working. New code in files of 100 lines or less; update CLI help and docs/DAEMON.md. (check: `grep -rq 'fn api_rejects_a_spoofed_role' rust/tirith && grep -rq 'fn api_rejects_calls_without_token' rust/tirith && cargo test -q -p tirith api_rejects`)
- [x] A per-bot secret store only the daemon and owner CLI can use. Add tirith secret set|list|remove <bot> <NAME>, reading set values from stdin and never from argv; list shows names only. Behind one small backend used in tests with a fake store, values go to the OS credential store when available (macOS Keychain service tirith.bot.<id>, Windows Credential Manager, Linux Secret Service) or an encrypted 0600 per-bot fallback keyed by protected daemon key material where no OS store exists. Daemon-internal lookup is by bot and name; a bot, lane or manager caller token can never read or enumerate values, including its own. Deleting a bot removes its stored values with its role. This item does not put secrets in a bot's process environment. Test bot_secrets_are_scoped proves bot A cannot reach bot B's value, argv and list expose no value, and deletion removes only that bot's entries. New code in files of 100 lines or less; update CLI help and docs/DAEMON.md. (check: `grep -rq 'fn bot_secrets_are_scoped' rust/tirith && cargo test -q -p tirith bot_secrets_are_scoped`)

## 37. Connectors through an MCP gateway 2/7: definitions, grants and policy

The connector is defined once; what each bot may do with it belongs under that bot's own role.
- [x] Define connectors and per-bot grants in ~/.tirith/config.toml, outside every repo. A top-level [[connectors]] has id, either command as an argv array or url, optional blocked (default false), deny and approval glob lists, and secrets as variable names only. A bot is granted a connector only when [roles.bot-<id>.connectors.<connector>] exists; that table may add tools, deny and approval lists but can only narrow the connector. An empty grant means the connector as defined. Add connectors to factory/keys.rs ROLE_KEYS and validate the top-level connector shape and references strictly, so a typo or an unknown connector is an error, not silence. The bot writer/sweep must preserve grants on an ordinary bot-card save and remove them when the bot role is deleted. Implement one pure verdict used for listing and calling, in this exact order: connector blocked; connector deny; bot deny; outside bot tools when tools is set; connector approval; bot approval; otherwise run. A refusal can name the tools the bot may use without naming secrets. Tests bot_rules_cannot_loosen_the_connector and deleting_a_bot_drops_its_grants use fake tool catalogs and config fixtures with no model. Update docs/DAEMON.md with the exact TOML shape. (check: `grep -rq 'fn bot_rules_cannot_loosen_the_connector' rust/tirith && grep -rq 'fn deleting_a_bot_drops_its_grants' rust/tirith && cargo test -q -p tirith bot_rules_cannot_loosen_the_connector && cargo test -q -p tirith deleting_a_bot_drops_its_grants`)

## 38. Connectors through an MCP gateway 3/7: the gateway in each bot run

Every bot sees one MCP server, Tirith; Tirith alone talks to the real connector servers.
- [x] Add tirith mcp --bot <id>, a stdio MCP gateway for bot runs only. The process must prove the bot from its caller token and refuse a mismatched --bot. It loads only connectors granted under that bot's role, starts local command servers or connects to remote URL servers, lists only tools allowed by the stage-37 verdict, and rechecks that verdict on every tools/call rather than trusting the list. Connector secrets are fetched from the per-bot store and injected only into the downstream process environment or remote auth headers; they never enter the agent's environment, MCP arguments, transcript or captures. Remote traffic leaves through Tirith's proxy tagged as the bot, not as the daemon. Add the gateway to bot runs in the anthropic, Codex and Muse MCP dialects following models/browser_args.rs; do not add it to lanes or managers. Every attempt is recorded through the daemon in turns with time, bot, connector, tool, sha256 of canonical arguments and result (never content), and verdict ran, approval required, refused by deny, refused by scope or refused by block; an approval match does not run yet. Tests with fake stdio and HTTP MCP servers and no model: mcp_gateway_scopes_tools, gateway_hides_secrets and refused_calls_are_recorded, including repeated refused calls. New code in files of 100 lines or less; update CLI help and docs/DAEMON.md. (check: `grep -rq 'fn mcp_gateway_scopes_tools' rust/tirith && grep -rq 'fn gateway_hides_secrets' rust/tirith && grep -rq 'fn refused_calls_are_recorded' rust/tirith && cargo test -q -p tirith mcp_gateway_scopes_tools && cargo test -q -p tirith gateway_hides_secrets && cargo test -q -p tirith refused_calls_are_recorded`)

## 39. Connectors through an MCP gateway 4/7: approval bound to the call

An approval is a one-use permit for one exact connector call, not a general yes to the bot.
- [x] When a gateway call matches approval, it does not run: the daemon opens an approval card naming the bot, connector and tool with a short, secret-free summary of its arguments, records waiting for approval, and the bot turn ends. Only an authenticated owner answer can create a one-use permit keyed by sha256 of connector id, tool name and canonical arguments; a caller inside the sandbox cannot approve its own call by forging a role, card answer or gateway request. The next identical call consumes the permit and runs once; a changed argument, another tool or connector, or a second identical run needs a new card. A no answer creates no permit. The gateway and record show approved-and-ran distinctly from an ordinary run. Test approval_is_bound_to_the_call with a fake MCP server proves no run before yes, exactly one run after yes, no reuse, changed arguments ask again, and a sandboxed self-approval attempt is refused. Update docs/DAEMON.md. (check: `grep -rq 'fn approval_is_bound_to_the_call' rust/tirith && cargo test -q -p tirith approval_is_bound_to_the_call`)

## 40. Connectors through an MCP gateway 5/7: per-bot OAuth login

Two bots may use the same remote connector through different accounts.
- [x] Add tirith connector login <id> --bot <bot> for remote MCP servers. It performs OAuth discovery from the server's .well-known metadata, uses PKCE and state (and dynamic client registration when the metadata requires it), opens the sign-in in the owner's browser, receives the code only on a 127.0.0.1 daemon callback, and stores access and refresh values in that bot's secret store under reserved connector names. The gateway obtains and refreshes the token itself and sends it only to that connector; another bot's store and account are untouched. A local connector needs no login and continues to use the secret names in its definition. Document Asana https://mcp.asana.com/v2/mcp and Calendly https://mcp.calendly.com as the manual acceptance servers, but keep automated coverage offline. Update CLI help and docs/DAEMON.md. (check: `grep -rq 'fn connector_oauth_flow' rust/tirith && cargo test -q -p tirith connector_oauth_flow`)

## 43. Plan housekeeping: delete a whole step

A daemon-written way to retire old roadmap steps without editing the plan by hand.

It removes exactly that numbered step: its `## N. Title` heading, intro/prose and every done or open item, from its heading line to the next numbered step or end of file; content before the step, later steps and their numbers do not change (a gap is allowed, never renumber). Clean only the boundary blank lines the removal creates. It refuses without touching the file when the number is not all digits, the step does not exist, or any lane of that project and step is not merged (queued, running, waiting, merge-queued, merging, waiting-disk, failed or stopped), naming those lane ids; merged lane history does not block it. Tests plan_step_delete_removes_only_that_step (middle and last step; surrounding steps intact; plan add still works in a surviving later step and refuses the deleted number) and plan_step_delete_refuses_a_live_step (nonexistent and stopped-lane cases leave bytes unchanged). Add both to checks/plan.rs, update rust/tirith/assets/usage.txt, the README command list if it names plan commands, and docs/DAEMON.md. New code in files of 100 lines or less.
